Fast convolution:
```
def fft_convolve(a,b):
    n_out=len(a)+len(b)-1
    n=next_fast_len(n_out)
    fa=rfft(a,n); fb=rfft(b,n)
    c=irfft(fa*fb,n,n_out) # scipy irfft with n_out? Actually irfft(a, n) where n is transform length, then slice. For real: irfft(fa*fb, n)[:n_out]
    return c
```
For deterministic positive, small negative due to roundoff; use np.maximum(c,0) for scoring? If actual direct has no negative, clipping okay for proxy. Aggressive clipping can hide errors; but small negatives not affect max. Use `c = np.where(c > 0, c, 0.0)`.

score proxy:
```
def score_fft(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); a=np.maximum(a,0)
    s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12 return inf
    C=fft_conv(a,a)
    return 2*len(a)*C.max()/s/s
```

Normalize:
```
def normalize_p(a):
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s==0: return uniform
   return a/s
```

Soft objective and gradient:
We need functions that compute objective and gradient for L-BFGS or Adam. We can implement both primal gradient and log gradient.

For `scipy.optimize.minimize` with x = log_weights y. Let p_i=exp(y_i)/sum. Objective `obj(y)`. To avoid overflow/underflow, shift y: z=y - max(y); p=exp(z); p/=sum. Max y may drift; we can keep within by subtract max.

Objective using log-sum-exp:
```
def soft_conv_objective(y, beta):
   p=softmax(y)
   C=fft_conv(p,p)
   C=np.clip(C, 0, None)
   m=C.max()
   # logsumexp stable: logsum = m + log(sum(exp(beta*(C-m))))
   # To avoid large beta* range, if beta*m large? Use scaled.
   vals=beta*(C-m)
   vals[vals < -50] = -50  # or mask
   logZ = m + np.log(np.sum(np.exp(vals)))
   return beta*logZ? Actually derivative of logsumexp scaled by beta: L=β log∑ e^{C/s?}
```
Standard: L = logsumexp(β C). Derivative = β w where w_k = e^{β C_k}/∑e^{β C_k}. So return L = mβ + logsum exp(β(C-m)). For gradient, w as above.

But β C may overflow if β large and C~1e-4; choose β up to maybe 10000, βC~1, okay. If C up to 1, β 1e5 ->1e5 overflow. But target C small. For safety use stable logsumexp and cap exponent to 50 by subtracting max. Then L = β*m + logsum expr; derivatives use w normalized.

Gradient w.r.t y: Need g_y = (g_p - p * (p·g_p)) / p? Let p=exp(z). ∂p_i/∂y_j = p_i(δ_ij - p_j). Objective L(p). gradient_y_i = (1/p_i)(g_p_i - ∑_j p_j g_p_j). For nonnegative p positive. We can compute raw g_p from convolution derivative, then center and divide. But dividing by p can amplify. Alternatively, for L-BFGS in y, this is correct. If p has very small values, gradient large; may push support. Could use lower bound y_min e.g. log(1e-12) using L-BFGS bounds to avoid -inf. But p positive. Or use Adam in primal.

Compute g_p:
Given weights w length L (sum=1) derived from soft C. g_i = 2∑_k w_k p_{k-i} with k-i in [0,n-1]. Use reverse convolution as derived.
```
def gradient_from_weights(p, w):
   n=len(p); L=2*n-1
   wr = w[::-1]  # length L
   N=next_fast_len(L+n-1)
   conv = irfft(rfft(p,N)*rfft(wr,N), N)[:L]
   grad = 2.0 * conv[::-1]  # conv index L-1-i
   return grad
```

Constraint sum p=1 but p not constrained in y; raw derivative not zero. Good.

To keep sum not necessary because normalize each step; but projection? Use simplex projection of p-θgrad. Implementation:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    # O(n log n)
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,n+1))+1 - css >0)[0][-1]
    theta=(css[rho]-1)/(rho+1)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0)
```
This projects Euclidean onto sum1,p≥0. Step: p_new=project_simplex(p - η grad). If grad has mean positive, projection adjusts. This may push to boundary. Repeat with line search.

Given target improvement small, local optimization likely enough. Let's focus code robust.

Potential algorithms:
A. Softmax objective + L-BFGS-B on log weights with continuation and random perturbations.
B. Multiplicative updates (exponentiated gradient) for many steps.
C. LP-like direction? maybe optional.
D. Support search: zero-out small probabilities to sidestep, or reinitialize support from random top sums.

Since available CPU 1, time 2400s, n=5582. We can do many iterations.

Let's design a practical loop:

1. Load parent `height_sequence_1` if available; normalize to sum1. If not, create initial sequences.
2. Evaluate parent actual score; update best.
3. Use a set of candidate generators:
   - parent
   - parent perturbations (Gaussian on log p)

def softmax_prob(y):

Top-level `fft_self_conv(p, N=None)`:
```
N = next_fast_len(2*n-1)
P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
C = np.fft.irfft(P*P, N)[:2*n-1]
C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
return C
```
Use `scipy.fft` maybe faster: `from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft`. We'll import. To avoid repeated `next_fast_len`, pass N maybe. We'll write:
```
def fft_self_conv(p, transform_len=None):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    if transform_len is None: transform_len=next_fast_len(L)
    F=rfft(p, transform_len)
    C=irfft(F*F, transform_len)[:L]
    C[C<0] = 0.0
    return C
```
But modifying input? okay.

    # vals <=0

```
def peak_weights(C, frac=0.05, beta_delta=None):
    maxC=C.max()
    if maxC <=0: return uniform
    if beta_delta is None: delta = max(maxC*0.05, 1e-12)
    vals=(C-maxC)/delta
    # vals <=0
    e=np.exp(np.clip(vals,-50,0))
    w=e/e.sum()
    return w
```

Line search for multiplicative:
```
def accept_step(p, raw_grad, alpha):
    scale=raw_grad.max()
    if scale <= 1e-300: return None
    # cap exponent to avoid underflow: max_exp = -alpha; ensure alpha<=50
    exponent = - (alpha/scale)*raw_grad
    exponent = np.clip(exponent, -50, 0)
    q=p*np.exp(exponent)
    q=q/q.sum()
    return q
```

Let's detail local functions.

`score_array` with optional `use_small_direct`? For small n, `np.convolve` may be exact; FFT okay. Use FFT always. If n small (<512), direct maybe overhead less; not needed.

`grad_weights` for multiplicative and L-BFGS. Need cache transform length for each n to save. Since global no closures, we can use a dict `_TRANS_CACHE = {}`. But top-level global mutable okay. But if multiple n, recompute.

```
_TRANS_CACHE = {}
def get_transform_len(n):
    L=2*n-1
    key=n
    if key not in _TRANS_CACHE:
       # For self conv length L, for grad length L+n-1

```
def take_multiplicative_step(p, grad, alpha):
    scale=grad.max()
    if scale<=1e-300: return None
    exp_arg = - (alpha/scale)*grad
    exp_arg = np.maximum(exp_arg, -50.0) # no underflow
    q=p*np.exp(exp_arg)
    s=q.sum()
    if s==0: return p
    return q/s
```

```
def _lbfgs_fun(y, beta, offset):
    p = softmax_probs(y)
    C = fft_self_conv(p)
    vals = beta*(C - offset)
    maxv = float(np.max(vals))
    e = np.exp(vals - maxv)
    z = float(np.sum(e))
    obj = float(maxv + np.log(z))  # logsumexp vals
    w = e / z
    grad_b = 2.0 * gradient_from_weights(p, w)  # gradient of ∑w C
    grad_b = beta * grad_b
    p_safe = np.maximum(p, 1e-300)
    center = float(np.dot(p, grad_b))
    grad_y = (grad_b - center*p) / p_safe
    grad_y = np.nan_to_num(grad_y, nan=0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)
    grad_y = np.clip(grad_y, -1e6, 1e6)
    return obj, grad_y
```
But `obj` = logsumexp(β(C-offset)), not multiplied by 1? Yes. Derivative = β∑w ∂C. Good.

If offset can be negative? M positive. offset maybe target_M positive.

Small improvement: For objective value only (L-BFGS uses obj for line search), the logsumexp of negative differences may be large negative if all below offset. L-BFGS still tries. Use `obj` finite.

If p has values near 0, log floor 1e-300 ~ -690, outside bounds; L-BFGS would project to lower -50. This creates small positive mass everywhere. Could be okay. But if optimal support sparse, lower -50 means p_min ~1.9e-22, negligible. Use bounds (-50, 20). For n large, total floor mass n*1e-22 negligible. Good. If p has exact zeros, y set to -50. We can initialize floor: `p_pos = np.maximum(p, 1e-20); p_pos normalized`, then log. But adding 1e-20 to zeros sum negligible. Good.

def run_lbfgs_once(y, beta, offset, maxiter):
    bounds = ((-50.0, 20.0),) * len(y)
    res = minimize(_lbfgs_fun, y, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(beta, offset), bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'maxfun': 2*maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxls':20, 'iprint':0})
    return res.x
```

       _BOUNDS_CACHE[n] = ((-50.0, 20.0),)*n
   return _BOUNDS_CACHE[n]
```

```
def active_weights(C, frac=0.01, min_count=3):
    M=C.max()
    if M<=0: return np.ones(len(C))/len(C)
    tol = max(M*frac, 1e-15)
    mask = C >= M-tol
    if np.count_nonzero(mask) < min_count:
         # top min_count
         idx = np.argpartition(C, -min_count)[-min_count:]
         weights uniform over idx? If min_count maybe.
         w=np.zeros_like(C); w[idx]=1.0; return w/w.sum()
    w=np.where(mask,1.0,0.0); return w/w.sum()
```

```

```

   n=len(p); best=p; best_score=score_fft(p); M0=...

```
def resample_sequence(a, n):
   if len(a)==n: return normalize_one(a)
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,len(a))
   x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   vals=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
   vals[vals<0]=0
   # maybe interpolate in log space for sparse? If many zeros, linear gives zero between. okay.
   return normalize_one(vals)
```

```
def make_initials(parent, n, rng):
   list=[]
   list.append(resample(parent,n) if parent is not None else uniform)
   list.append(np.ones(n))
   # exponentials
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   for c in [-4,-2,-1,0,1,2,4]:
       list.append(np.exp(c*x)); list.append(np.exp(c*(1-x)))
   for eps in [0.2,0.5,0.8]:
      for phase in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi, 3*np.pi/2]:
         list.append(1+eps*np.sin(2*np.pi*x+phase))
   list.append(rng.exponential(1,n)+0.1)
   list.append(rng.gamma(1.5,1,n)+0.1)
   # block random
   for block in [8,32]:
       blocks = max(1,n//block)
       amps = rng.exponential(1, blocks)
       seq = np.repeat(amps, block)[:n]
       list.append(seq)
   return list
```

Let's write `run_multiplicative` carefully.

```
def run_multiplicative(p, deadline, rng, max_iters=200000):
    n=len(p)
    best=np.array(p,float); best_M=fft_self_conv(best).max(); best_score=2*n*best_M
    p=np.array(best,float)
    alpha=0.5
    stuck=0
    frac_cycle=(0.001,0.005,0.02,0.1)
    for it in range(max_iters):
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now > deadline: break
       C=fft_self_conv(p)
       M=float(C.max())
       if not np.isfinite(M): break
       cur_score=2*n*M
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=cur_score; best=p.copy(); best_M=M; stuck=0
       if best_M <= lower_m*1.000001: break
       if M - best_M < 1e-14 and it>100: # not current
          pass
       if M <= 1e-15: break
       frac=frac_cycle[it % len(frac_cycle)]
       w=soft_weights(C, frac)
       grad=gradient_from_weights(p,w)
       scale=float(grad.max())
       if scale <= 1e-300: break
       cur=p
       step_alpha=alpha
       improved=False
       # We need use current score not best. If q score < cur_score
       for trial in range(4):
           q=multi_step(p,grad,step_alpha)
           Cq=fft_self_conv(q)
           Mq=float(Cq.max())
           if np.isfinite(Mq) and Mq < M - 1e-12:
               p=q
               improved=True
               alpha=min(alpha*1.1, 20.0)
               break
           step_alpha*=0.5
           if step_alpha < 1e-4: break
       if improved:
           stuck=0
       else:
           stuck += 1
           alpha = max(alpha*0.5, 1e-3)
           if stuck > 25:
              # perturb
              mix=0.05 + 0.1*min(1, stuck/100)
              noise = rng.random(n)  # or normal
              p = (1-mix)*p + mix*noise
              p = normalize_one(p)
              alpha=0.3
              stuck=0
       if it % 1000 ==0: print(...)
    return best, best_score
```
The line search compares q score to current M. It uses best_score global. If a step slightly increases current but still below best? not possible if best<=current. We accept only decrease. Good.

Soft weights `soft_weights(C, frac)`: If frac very small, only top; if flat, broad.
```
def soft_weights(C, frac):
    M=C.max()
    if M<=0: return np.ones_like(C)/len(C)
    delta=max(M*frac, 1e-18)
    vals=(C-M)/delta
    vals=np.clip(vals, -50.0, 0.0)
    e=np.exp(vals)
    s=e.sum()
    if s==0: e[np.argmax(C)]=1; s=1
    return e/s
```
Maybe use top uniform if s too small? soft okay.

Multiplicative step:
```
def multiplicative_step(p, grad, alpha):
    scale=grad.max()
    if scale<=0: return p.copy()
    ex = -(alpha/scale)*grad
    ex = np.maximum(ex, -50.0)
    q=p*np.exp(ex)
    s=q.sum()
    if s<=0: return p.copy()
    return q/s
```
If p has zeros, they stay. Perturb adds zeros to positive if noise positive. Good.

Note: `grad` entries may be extremely small for positions not paired with peaks; exponent -0 for them, relative increase when normalized. This can fill zeros? If p_i=0, p*exp=0, no fill. Perturb periodic.

Potential issue: The objective is nonconvex; multiplicative line search with soft weights may get stuck at a local KKT. L-BFGS might escape if perturbed.

def perturb_sequence(p, sigma, rng):
    y=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-12))
    y += sigma * rng.normal(size=len(p))
    y = y - y.max()
    q=np.exp(np.clip(y,-50,50))
    return normalize_one(q)
```

def softmax_probs(y):
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    y=np.clip(y, -60, 60)
    y = y - y.max()
    p=np.exp(y)
    s=p.sum()
    if s == 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       p=np.full(len(y), 1.0/len(y)); return p
    return p/s
```

2. `_TRANS_CACHE = {}`. Function:
```
def get_transform_info(n):
    key=n
    if key not in _TRANS_CACHE:
       N = 1 << (2*n - 2).bit_length()
       L = 2*n - 1
       _TRANS_CACHE[key] = (N,L)
    return _TRANS_CACHE[key]
```
For n=0? not called. For n=1, 0 bit_length 0 -> N=1,L=1.

3. `fft_self_conv(p)`:
```
N,L=get_transform_info(n)
F=rfft(p,N)
C=irfft(F*F,N)
C=C[:L]
C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
return C
```
`irfft` with N returns real length N. Good. For N=1, rfft length1.

4. `gradient_from_weights(p,w)`:
```
N,L=...
wr = w[::-1]
Fp=rfft(p,N); Fw=rfft(wr,N)
G=irfft(Fp*Fw,N)
seg = G[n-1:L]
grad = 2.0 * seg[::-1]
grad = np.maximum(grad, 0.0)
return grad
```
If ceiling w length >N? N≥L. For n=1, w length1, seg G[0:1], reverse. Good.

Could there be alias for seg if N only >2n-1 but <3n-2? We reasoned safe. Need test with n=3, N=8, L=5, full gradient length 7, needed indices 4,3,2. a=np.maximum(a,0)
s=a.sum()
if s<1e-300: return np.full(len(a) if a.size else 1, 1.0/(max(len(a),1)))
return a/s
```

8. `soft_weights(C, frac)`:

M=C.max(); if M<=0: return np.full(len(C),1/len(C))
delta=max(M*frac, 1e-300)
vals=(C-M)/delta
vals=np.clip(vals,-60,0)
e=np.exp(vals); s=e.sum()
if s<=0: w=zeros; w[argmax]=1; return
return e/s
```

11. `lbfgs_objective(y, beta, offset)`:
Need to avoid expensive allocation? okay.
`C = fft_self_conv(p)`. If C max <=0? objective -inf? But p positive sum1, max>0.
Weights: use `vals = beta * (C - offset)`, stable. If `beta` huge and (C-offset) range large, `vals-maxv` small negative. Good.
`grad_p = gradient_from_weights(p,w) * beta`. Note gradient_from_weights includes 2. Good.
`center = np.dot(p, grad_p)`; if p has zeros? p positive due softmax. `grad_y=(grad_p - center*p)/p`. Since p positive. Clip finite.
Return `float(obj), grad_y.astype(float)`.

Potential issue: The objective is logsumexp of β(C-offset), not scaled by 1/β? If β large, objective changes scale; line search may step small. But gradient scaled by β. It's equivalent to minimizing logsumexp. Good.

Maybe for offset target, if offset very low (near lower bound), C-offset ~1e-4, β maybe 50, vals~0.005, L small. L-BFGS works.

12. `run_lbfgs(p, deadline, target_score, rng, max_calls=20, maxiter=25)`:
Pseudo:
```
def run_lbfgs(p, deadline, target_score, rng):
    n=len(p); p=normalize_one(p); best=p; C=fft_self_conv(p); best_M=C.max(); best_score=2*n*best_M
    calls=0; stuck=0
    while calls < max_calls and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
        M = best_M
        lower_M = 1.001/(2*n)
        if 2*n*M > target_score:
           target_M = max(target_score/(2*n), lower_M)
        else:
           target_M = max(lower_M, M*0.985)
        if M <= target_M*1.0001: break
        # choose offsets from easier to harder
        offsets=[]
        offsets.append(max(target_M, M*0.995))
        offsets.append(max(target_M, M*0.98))
        offsets.append(max(target_M, M*0.96))
        improved=False
        for offset in offsets:
            if offset >= M: continue
            diff = max(M-offset, M*0.002)
            beta = max(20.0, 3.0/diff)
            beta = min(beta, 5000.0) # for M=1e-4,diff=2e-6 beta 1500, cap ok
            for btry in (beta*0.25, beta, beta*3):
                btry=min(max(float(btry),1.0),20000.0)
                y=np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-20))
                y = y - y.mean()
                try:
                   res=minimize(_lbfgs_objective, y, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(btry, offset), bounds=get_bounds(n), options={...})
                   y_new=np.asarray(res.x,float)
                   p_new=softmax_probs(y_new)
                   C_new=fft_self_conv(p_new)
                   M_new=float(C_new.max())
                   calls+=1
                   if np.isfinite(M_new) and M_new < M - 1e-15:
                       p=p_new; M=M_new; best=p.copy(); best_M=M; best_score=2n*M; improved=True
                       break
                except Exception:
                   calls+=1
            if improved: break
        if not improved:
            stuck+=1
            # perturb p
            mix=0.05
            p = (1-mix)*best + mix*rng.random(n)
            p=normalize_one(p)
            C=fft_self_conv(p); M=C.max()
            if M < best_M: update
            else:
                # if stuck several times, break
                if stuck >=3: break
    return best, best_score
```
This starts L-BFGS from `p` (maybe perturbed) not from best if no improvement. If improved, p best. If STUCK, perturbs best and sets p to perturbed. If next calls fail, stuck 2,3 then break. Good.

But if p perturbed has higher M, offsets computed from best_M still; M in loop `M=best_M` not p's M. L-BFGS starts from p but objective offset based on best_M, fine. For condition `offset >= M`, use best_M.

Maybe better to set `M = best_M` each loop.

`get_bounds(n)` top-level.

The bounds lower -50 may be too high if p has zeros and l-BFGS wants zero; okay. Upper 20.

`maxiter` in options: 20? Use 30, maxfun 200. For n=10000, maybe 2s per call, 20 calls 40s. Budget controlled.

Could there be `minimize` memory issue bounds tuple length n for 10000? fine.

13. `refine_candidate(p, deadline, rng, target_score)`:
```
def refine_candidate(p, deadline, rng, target_score):
   n=len(p); best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(p)
   # Multiplicative first 35%
   mult_deadline = min(time.monotonic() + (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.35, deadline-30)
   p_m, sc_m = run_multiplicative(p, max(time.monotonic()+10, mult_deadline), rng)
   if sc_m < best_score: update
   # If time left > L-BFGS min
   if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60:
      p_l, sc_l = run_lbfgs(p_m, deadline-30, target_score, rng, max_calls=15, maxiter=20)
      if sc_l < best_score: update
   # Final multiplicative for remaining
   if time.monotonic() < deadline-10:
      p_f, sc_f = run_multiplicative(best, deadline-5, rng, max_iters=20000)

def resample_sequence(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).reshape(-1); n=int(n)
   if a.size==n: return normalize_one(a)
   x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
   x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   # If many zeros, linear interpolation might produce spikes? okay.
   vals=np.interp(x_new,x_old,a)
   vals[~np.isfinite(vals)] = 0
   vals[vals<0] = 0
   # Add tiny floor to all to allow lbfgs? normalize_one not change zeros; but y floor handles.
   return normalize_one(vals)
```

def noise_perturb(p, sigma, rng, mix=1.0):
   y=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-12))
   y = y - y.mean()
   y = y + sigma*rng.standard_normal(len(p))
   y = np.clip(y, -50, 50)
   q=np.exp(y); return normalize_one(q)
```

`run_lbfgs`: We need import `minimize`. Catch exceptions if SciPy not available. If no minimize, return p.

```
def run_lbfgs(p, deadline, target_score, rng, max_calls=None):
    n=len(p); p=normalize_one(p)
    C=fft_self_conv(p); best_M=float(C.max()); p_best=p.copy(); best_score=2*n*best_M
    if max_calls is None:
       max_calls = 14 if n <= 6000 else (10 if n <= 9000 else 6)
    maxiter = 14 if n > 7000 else 18
    stuck=0; calls=0
    while calls<max_calls and time.monotonic() < deadline - 40:
       M=best_M
       lower_M=1.001/(2*n)
       if 2*n*M > target_score:
          target_M=max(target_score/(2*n), lower_M)
       else:
          target_M=max(lower_M, M*0.985)
       if M <= target_M*1.0001: break
       offsets=[]
       offsets.append(max(target_M, M*0.995))
       offsets.append(max(target_M, M*0.98))
       offsets.append(max(target_M, M*0.96))
       improved=False
       for offset in offsets:
          if offset >= M*0.9999: continue
          diff=M-offset
          beta=max(20.0, 4.0/max(diff, M*0.002))
          beta=min(beta, 8000.0)
          btrials=[beta*0.25, beta, beta*3]
          for b in btrials:
             if calls>=max_calls or time.monotonic() > deadline-40: break
             y=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-20)); y=y-y.mean()
             try:
                res=minimize(_lbfgs_objective, y, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(b, offset), bounds=get_bounds(n), options={'maxiter':maxiter, 'maxfun': 5*maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-6,'maxls':10,'iprint':0})
                y_new=res.x
                p_new=softmax_probs(y_new)
                Cn=fft_self_conv(p_new)
                Mn=float(Cn.max())
             except Exception:
                Mn=np.inf
             calls += 1
             if np.isfinite(Mn) and Mn < M - 1e-15:
                 p=p_new; M=Mn; p_best=p_new.copy(); best_M=Mn; best_score=2*n*Mn; improved=True
                 break
          if improved: break
       if not improved:
          stuck+=1
          # Perturb best
          mix=0.03 + 0.05*min(1,stuck)
          mode=int(rng.integers(0,3))
          if mode==0:
             p = normalize_one((1-mix)*p_best + mix*rng.random(n))
          elif mode==1:
             p = normalize_one(p_best[rng.permutation(n)])
          else:
             p = noise_perturb(p_best, 0.2, rng)
          C=fft_self_conv(p); M=float(C.max())
          if M < best_M: update
          if stuck>=3: break
    return p_best, best_score
```
Note: `p` for next L-BFGS after improvement set to `p_new`; after non-improvement perturbation set to perturbed. Good. `run_multiplicative`:
```
def run_multiplicative(p, deadline, rng, max_iters=500000):
    n=len(p); p=normalize_one(p)
    C=fft_self_conv(p); best_M=float(C.max()); p_best=p.copy(); best_score=2*n*best_M
    alpha=1.0; stuck=0
    fracs=[0.001,0.003,0.01,0.03,0.1]
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       C=fft_self_conv(p); M=float(C.max())
       if np.isfinite(M) and M < best_M - 1e-15:
           best_M=M; p_best=p.copy(); best_score=2*n*M_M? careful
       if best_M <= 1.001/(2*n): break
       if M <= 1e-15: break
       frac=fracs[it % len(fracs)]
       w=soft_weights(C, frac)
       g=gradient_from_weights(p,w)
       c=np.dot(p,g)
       d=g-c
       scale=float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
       if scale <= 1e-15:
          stuck+=1
          if stuck>20: break
          continue
       cur_score=2*n*M
       improved=False
       a=alpha
       for _ in range(4):
           q=exp_step_centered(p,d,a,scale)
           Cq=fft_self_conv(q)
           Mq=float(Cq.max())
           if np.isfinite(Mq) and Mq < M - 1e-15:
              p=q; improved=True; alpha=min(alpha*1.15, 15.0)
              break
           a*=0.5
           if a < 1e-3: break
       if improved:
          stuck=0
       else:
          stuck+=1
          alpha=max(alpha*0.6,0.02)
          if stuck > 20:
             mode=int(rng.integers(0,3))
             if mode==0: p=noise_perturb(p, 0.15, rng)
             elif mode==1: p=normalize_one(p[rng.permutation(n)])
             else: p=normalize_one(0.85*p + 0.15*rng.random(n))
             alpha=0.5; stuck=0
       if it % 5000 ==0:
          print(f"  mult n={n} iter={it} M={M:.8e} best_M={best_M:.8e}")
    return p_best, best_score
```
Need define `exp_step_centered(top-level)`:
```
def step_centered(p,d,alpha,scale):
   ex=-(alpha/scale)*d
   ex=np.clip(ex,-50.0,50.0)
   q=p*np.exp(ex)
   s=q.sum()
   if s<=0: return p.copy()
   return q/s
```

If scale large? alpha/scale keeps max exponent <=alpha. Good.

Potential issue: if d has negative values with magnitude similar, exponent positive up to alpha, can increase some variables. Clip 50. Good.

```
def refine_candidate(p, deadline, rng, target_score):
    p = normalize_one(np.asarray(p,float))
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(p)
    now=time.monotonic()
    # first mult
    if now < deadline - 30:
       mult_dl = min(now + (deadline-now)*0.30, deadline-30)
       p1, sc1 = run_multiplicative(p, max(now+1, mult_dl), rng)
       if sc1 < best_score: best=p1; best_score=sc1
    if now < deadline - 60:
       p2, sc2 = run_lbfgs(best, deadline-60, target_score, rng)
       if sc2 < best_score: best=p2; best_score=sc2
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
       p3, sc3 = run_multiplicative(best, deadline-5, rng, max_iters=20000)
       if sc3 < best_score: best=p3; best_score=sc3
    return best, best_score
```

```python
"""
Multi-stage non-negative coefficient search for minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

The code uses:
  - FFT-based convolution scoring and gradients,
  - exponentiated-gradient multiplicative balancing of active convolution peaks,
  - L-BFGS-B continuation in log-softmax variables with violation-based soft objectives,
  - diverse restarts / perturbations,
  - occasional length changes,
  - final validation against the provided evaluate_sequence() oracle.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None

_TRANS_CACHE = {}
_BOUNDS_CACHE = {}


def get_transform_info(n):
    """Return (transform_length, full_convolution_length)."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return (1, 1)
    if n not in _TRANS_CACHE:
        # Power of two at least 2n-1.  This is enough for both self-convolution
        # and the needed segment of the weighted gradient convolution.
        N = 1 << (2 * n - 2).bit_length()
        L = 2 * n - 1
        _TRANS_CACHE[n] = (N, L)
    return _TRANS_CACHE[n]


def get_bounds(n):
    """Bounds for log-weights used by L-BFGS-B."""
    n = int(n)
    if n not in _BOUNDS_CACHE:
        _BOUNDS_CACHE[n] = ((-50.0, 20.0),) * n
    return _BOUNDS_CACHE[n]


def normalize_one(a):
    """Clip to nonnegative finite values and normalize to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def fft_self_conv(p):
    """Linear self-convolution of a nonnegative vector, clipped at 0."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    N, L = get_transform_info(n)
    F = rfft(p, N)
    C = irfft(F * F, N)
    C = C[:L]
    C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
    return C


def gradient_from_weights(p, w):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k C_k wrt p_i, where C = conv(p,p).

    Returns vector of length n:
        2 * sum_k w_k p_{k-i}
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    N, L = get_transform_info(n)

    wr = w[::-1]
    Fp = rfft(p, N)
    Fw = rfft(wr, N)
    G = irfft(Fp * Fw, N)

    # We need indices L-1-i for i=0..n-1, i.e. G[n-1:L] reversed.
    seg = G[n - 1:L]
    grad = 2.0 * seg[::-1]
    grad = np.maximum(grad, 0.0)
    return grad


def score_fft(p):
    """Fast proxy score: 2*n*max(conv(p,p)) for sum(p)=1."""
    p = normalize_one(p)
    n = p.size
    C = fft_self_conv(p)
    m = float(C.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def soft_weights(C, frac):
    """
    Soft weights over convolution coefficients, concentrating on peaks.
    frac controls the relative bandwidth around the maximum.
    """
    C = np.asarray(C, dtype=np.float64)
    M = float(C.max())
    L = C.size
    if M <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)

    delta = max(M * frac, 1e-300)
    vals = (C - M) / delta
    vals = np.clip(vals, -70.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(vals)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(C))] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / s


def centered_gradient(p, w):
    """
    Centered gradient of sum_k w_k C_k on the probability simplex:
        g - <p,g>
    """
    g = gradient_from_weights(p, w)
    c = float(np.dot(p, g))
    return g - c


def multiplicative_step_centered(p, d, alpha, scale):
    """
    Exponentiated-generate step:
        p_new ∝ p * exp(-alpha * d / scale)
    """
    if scale <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    ex = -(float(alpha) / float(scale)) * d
    ex = np.clip(ex, -50.0, 50.0)
    q = p * np.exp(ex)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return p.copy()
    return q / s


def run_multiplicative(p, deadline, rng, max_iters=500000):
    """
    Exponentiated-gradient balancing for active peaks.
    Returns (best_p, best_proxy_score).
    """
    p = normalize_one(p)
    n = p.size
    C = fft_self_conv(p)
    best_M = float(C.max())
    best_score = float(2.0 * n * best_M)
    best_p = p.copy()

    if not np.isfinite(best_M) or best_M <= 0:
        return best_p, best_score

    alpha = 1.0
    stuck = 0
    fracs = (0.001, 0.003, 0.010, 0.030, 0.100)
    lower_M = 1.0005 / (2.0 * n)

    for it in range(int(max_iters)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        C = fft_self_conv(p)
        M = float(C.max())
        if not np.isfinite(M):
            break

        if M < best_M - 1e-15:
            best_M = M
            best_score = float(2.0 * n * M)
            best_p = p.copy()

        if best_M <= lower_M:
            break
        if M <= 1e-15:
            break

        frac = fracs[it % len(fracs)]
        w = soft_weights(C, frac)
        d = centered_gradient(p, w)
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))

        if scale <= 1e-15:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck > 25:
                break
            continue

        improved = False
        a = alpha

        for _trial in range(4):
            q = multiplicative_step_centered(p, d, a, scale)
            Cq = fft_self_conv(q)
            Mq = float(Cq.max())

            if np.isfinite(Mq) and Mq < M - 1e-15:
                p = q
                if Mq < best_M - 1e-15:
                    best_M = Mq
                    best_score = float(2.0 * best_score * (Mq / best_M))
                    best_p = q.copy()
                improved = True
                alpha = min(alpha * 1.15, 20.0)
                break

            a *= 0.5
            if a < 1e-3:
                break

        if improved:
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1
            alpha = max(alpha * 0.6, 0.02)

            if stuck > 20:
                mode = int(rng.integers(0, 3))
                if mode == 0:
                    y = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-12))
                    y = y - float(y.mean())
                    y = y + 0.15 * rng.standard_normal(n)
                    y = np.clip(y, -50.0, 50.0)
                    p = normalize_one(np.exp(y))
                elif mode == 1:
                    p = normalize_one(p[rng.permutation(n)])
                else:
                    p = normalize_one(0.85 * p + 0.15 * rng.random(n))

                alpha = 0.5
                stuck = 0

        if it % 5000 == 0:
            print(f"  multiplicative n={n} iter={it} M={M:.8e} best_M={best_M:.8e}")

    return best_p, best_score


def softmax_probs(y):
    """Stable softmax -> probability vector."""
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=60.0, neginf=-60.0)
    y = np.clip(y, -60.0, 60.0)
    y = y - float(y.max())
    p = np.exp(y)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(y.shape, 1.0 / max(1, y.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s


def lbfgs_objective(y, beta, offset):
    """
    Objective and gradient in log-softmax variables.

    Objective:
        L = logsumexp_{k: C_k > offset} ( beta * (C_k - offset) )

    This focuses the descent on coefficients currently above `offset`.
    """
    p = softmax_probs(y)
    C = fft_self_conv(p)

    v = C - float(offset)
    active = v > 0.0

    if not np.any(active):
        return (0.0, np.zeros_like(y, dtype=np.float64))

    va = v[active]
    maxv = float(va.max())
    if maxv <= 1e-300:
        return (0.0, np.zeros_like(y, dtype=np.float64))

    e = np.exp(float(beta) * (va - maxv))
    z = float(e.sum())
    if z <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(z):
        w = np.zeros_like(C, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(C))] = 1.0
        obj = float(beta) * maxv
    else:
        wvals = e / z
        w = np.zeros_like(C, dtype=np.float64)
        w[active] = wvals
        obj = float(beta) * maxv + float(np.log(z))

    g = gradient_from_weights(p, w)
    grad_p = float(beta) * g
    c = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))

    grad_y = p * (grad_p - c)
    grad_y = np.nan_to_num(grad_y, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=-1e6)
    grad_y = np.clip(grad_y, -1e6, 1e6)

    return (float(obj), grad_y)


def run_lbfgs(p, deadline, target_score, rng, max_calls=None):
    """
    L-BFGS-B continuation in log-softmax variables, using violation offsets.
    Returns (best_p, best_proxy_score).
    """
    p = normalize_one(p)
    n = p.size

    C = fft_self_conv(p)
    best_M = float(C.max())
    best_score = float(2.0 * n * best_M)
    best_p = p.copy()

    if minimize is None:
        return best_p, best_score

    if max_calls is None:
        if n <= 6000:
            max_calls = 14
        elif n <= 9000:
            max_calls = 10
        else:
            max_calls = 6

    maxiter = 14 if n > 7000 else 18
    calls = 0
    stuck = 0
    lower_M = 1.0005 / (2.0 * n)

    while calls < int(max_calls) and time.monotonic() < deadline - 40:
        M = best_M
        if 2.0 * n * M > target_score:
            target_M = max(target_score / (2.0 * n), lower_M)
        else:
            target_M = max(lower_M, M * 0.985)

        if M <= target_M * 1.0001:
            break

        # Try easier offsets first; if impossible, better perturb than overforcing.
        offsets = [
            max(target_M, M * 0.995),
            max(target_M, M * 0.980),
            max(target_M, M * 0.960),
        ]

        improved = False
        for offset in offsets:
            if offset >= M * 0.9999:
                continue

            diff = max(M - offset, 1e-300)
            base_beta = 10.0 / diff
            base_beta = max(10.0, min(500000.0, base_beta))
            btrials = (base_beta * 0.25, base_beta, base_beta * 3.0)

            for b in btrials:
                if calls >= max_calls or time.monotonic() > deadline - 40:
                    break

                y = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-20))
                y = y - float(y.mean())

                try:
                    res = minimize(
                        lbfgs_objective,
                        y,
                        method="L-BFGS-B",
                        jac=True,
                        args=(float(b), float(offset)),
                        bounds=get_bounds(n),
                        options={
                            "maxiter": int(maxiter),
                            "maxfun": int(5 * maxiter),
                            "ftol": 1e-12,
                            "gtol": 1e-6,
                            "maxls": 10,
                            "iprint": 0,
                        },
                    )
                    y_new = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
                    p_new = softmax_probs(y_new)
                    Cn = fft_self_conv(p_new)
                    Mn = float(Cn.max())
                except Exception:
                    Mn = float("inf")

                calls += 1

                if np.isfinite(Mn) and Mn < M - 1e-15:
                    p = p_new
                    M = Mn
                    best_M = Mn
                    best_score = float(2.0 * n * M)
                    best_p = p_new.copy()
                    improved = True
                    break

            if improved:
                break

        if not improved:
            stuck += 1
            mix = 0.04 + 0.05 * min(1.0, float(stuck))
            mode = int(rng.integers(0, 3))

            if mode == 0:
                p = normalize_one((1.0 - mix) * best_p + mix * rng.random(n))
            elif mode == 1:
                p = normalize_one(best_p[rng.permutation(n)])
            else:
                y = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-12))
                y = y - float(y.mean())
                y = y + 0.25 * rng.standard_normal(n)
                y = np.clip(y, -50.0, 50.0)
                p = normalize_one(np.exp(y))

            C = fft_self_conv(p)
            M = float(C.max())
            if np.isfinite(M) and M < best_M - 1e-15:
                best_M = M
                best_score = float(2.0 * n * M)
                best_p = p.copy()

            if stuck >= 3:
                break

    return best_p, best_score


def noise_perturb(p, sigma, rng, mix=None):
    """Log-noise perturbation of a nonnegative sequence."""
    p = normalize_one(p)
    n = p.size
    y = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-12))
    y = y - float(y.mean())
    y = y + float(sigma) * rng.standard_normal(n)
    y = np.clip(y, -50.0, 50.0)
    q = np.exp(y)
    if mix is None:
        return normalize_one(q)
    return normalize_one((1.0 - float(mix)) * p + float(mix) * q)


def resample_sequence(a, n):
    """Linear resample a normalized sequence to length n."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == n:
        return normalize_one(a)

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    vals = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    vals = np.where(np.isfinite(vals), vals, 0.0)
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 0.0)
    return normalize_one(vals)


def refine_candidate(p, deadline, rng, target_score):
    """
    Refine one candidate using a short multiplicative stage,
    the main L-BFGS continuation, and a final multiplicative polish.
    """
    p = normalize_one(p)
    cur_score = score_fft(p)
    best_p = p.copy()

    now = time.monotonic()
    if deadline <= now:
        return best_p, cur_score

    # 1) Fast multilative balancing.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
        mult_dl = min(now + (deadline - now) * 0.30, deadline - 30)
        p1, s1 = run_multiplicative(p, max(now + 1.0, mult_dl), rng)
        if s1 < cur_score - 1e-12:
            best_p = p1
            cur_score = s1

    # 2) L-BFGS continuation.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60:
        p2, s2 = run_lbfgs(best_p, deadline - 60, target_score, rng)
        if s2 < cur_score - 1e-12:
            best_p = p2
            cur_score = s2

    # 3) Final multiplicative polish.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
        p3, s3 = run_multiplicative(best_p, deadline - 5, rng, max_iters=20000)
        if s3 < cur_score - 1e-12:
            best_p = p3
            cur_score = s3

    return best_p, cur_score


def direct_score(seq):
    """Call the provided evaluate_sequence() oracle safely."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def consider_candidate(p, best_p, best_score, name):
    """
    Evaluate a candidate by proxy and by the real oracle (when possible).
    Returns (accepted_p, accepted_score).
    """
    p = normalize_one(p)
    proxy = score_fft(p)
    direct = direct_score(p.tolist())

    accepted_p = best_p
    accepted_score = best_score

    if np.isfinite(direct):
        if direct < best_score - 1e-12:
            accepted_p = p.copy()
            accepted_score = direct
    else:
        if proxy < best_score - 1e-12:
            accepted_p = p.copy()
            accepted_score = proxy

    direct_str = f"{direct:.9f}" if np.isfinite(direct) else "inf"
    score_str = f"{accepted_score:.9f}" if np.isfinite(accepted_score) else "inf"
    print(f"    {name}: proxy={proxy:.9f} direct={direct_str} accepted={score_str}")

    return accepted_p, accepted_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for a low-score sequence.

    Returns a list of nonnegative floats. The returned sequence is the best
    one seen by the real evaluator, or by the FFT proxy if the evaluator
    is unavailable/invalid.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    search_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, budget - 180.0)

    # Load parent if available.
    parent = None
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
        arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.ndim != 1:
            arr = arr.reshape(-1)
        if arr.size > 0:
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
            if arr.sum() > 0:
                parent = normalize_one(arr)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is None:
        parent = np.ones(5582, dtype=np.float64)
        parent = normalize_one(parent)

    n0 = int(parent.size)
    target = 1.5027

    best_p = parent.copy()
    proxy0 = score_fft(best_p)
    direct0 = direct_score(best_p.tolist())
    if np.isfinite(direct0):
        best_score = direct0
    else:
        best_score = proxy0

    print(f"parent n={n0} proxy={proxy0:.9f} direct={best_score:.9f}")

    # ---------------------------------------------
    # Phase 1: long refine of the parent / current best.
    # ---------------------------------------------
    parent_dl = min(t0 + 500.0, search_deadline - 60.0)
    if parent_dl > time.monotonic():
        print(f"phase 1: refine parent until t={parent_dl - t0:.1f}s")
        cand, _ = refine_candidate(best_p, parent_dl, rng, target)
        best_p, best_score = consider_candidate(cand, best_p, best_score, "parent-refined")

    # ---------------------------------------------
    # Phase 2: perturbations of current best, plus some diverse starts.
    # ---------------------------------------------
    print("phase 2: perturbations")
    idx = 0
    while time.monotonic() < search_deadline - 300:
        n = int(best_p.size)
        idx += 1

        # Create a new starting point.
        if idx % 4 == 1:
            sigma = 0.05 + 0.08 * (idx % 5)
            p_init = noise_perturb(best_p, sigma, rng)
            name = f"noise_s{sigma:.2f}_{idx}"
        elif idx % 4 == 2:
            p_perm = normalize_one(best_p[rng.permutation(n)])
            p_init = noise_perturb(p_perm, 0.05, rng)
            name = f"perm_noise_{idx}"
        elif idx % 4 == 3:
            # Random dense initial for this length.
            p_init = normalize_one(rng.exponential(1.0, n) + 0.1)
            name = f"random_gamma_{idx}"
        else:
            # Smoothed random ramp / blocks.
            if idx % 8 == 0:
                x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
                c = rng.uniform(-4.0, 4.0)
                p_init = normalize_one(np.exp(c * x) + 0.05)
            else:
                b = int(rng.integers(4, 129))
                nb = max(1, (n + b - 1) // b)
                amps = rng.exponential(1.0, nb)
                p_init = normalize_one(np.repeat(amps, b)[:n])
            name = f"structured_{idx}"

        p_init = normalize_one(p_init)
        init_score = score_fft(p_init)

        # Do not spend long on obviously very bad random starts.
        if idx % 4 >= 3 and finite_sm = init_score and finite_sm > best_score + 0.8:
            continue

        cand_dl = min(time.monotonic() + 80.0, search_deadline - 300)
        cand, _ = refine_candidate(p_init, cand_dl, rng, target)
        best_p, best_score = consider_candidate(cand, best_p, best_score, name)

    # ---------------------------------------------
    # Phase 3: try alternate lengths by resampling best parent/best.
    # ---------------------------------------------
    print("phase 3: alternate lengths")
    source_n = n0
    trials = [
        int(max(2048, source_n * 0.85)),
        8192,
        int(max(2048, source_n * 1.15)),
    ]
    seen = set()
    for n_trial in trials:
        n_trial = int(n_trial)
        if n_trial in seen:
            continue
        if n_trial < 2048 or n_trial > 12000:
            continue
        seen.add(n_trial)

        if time.monotonic() > search_deadline - 220:
            break

        p_init = resample_sequence(best_p, n_trial)
        if p_init.size != n_trial:
            p_init = resample_sequence(parent, n_trial)

        cand_dl = min(time.monotonic() + 140.0, search_deadline - 220)
        cand, _ = refine_candidate(p_init, cand_dl, rng, target)
        best_p, best_score = consider_candidate(cand, best_p, best_score, f"length_{n_trial}")

    # ---------------------------------------------
    # Phase 4: final full refinement of the global best.
    # ---------------------------------------------
    if time.monotonic() < search_deadline - 10:
        print("phase 4: final refinement of best")
        cand, _ = refine_candidate(best_p, search_deadline, rng, target)
        best_p, best_score = consider_candidate(cand, best_p, best_score, "final-refined")

    # Final safety: ensure finite, nonnegative, normalized, and return Python floats.
    out = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        out = np.ones(max(1, n0), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        out = out / s

    final_direct = direct_score(out.tolist())
    print(f"FINAL n={out.size} direct={final_direct:.9f} tracked={best_score:.9f}")

    if np.isfinite(final_direct) and final_direct < best_score:
        best_score = final_direct

    # If for some reason the tracked sequence is invalid, fall back to parent.
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        fallback = parent.copy()
        fb_s = fallback.sum()
        if fb_s > 1e-300:
            fallback = fallback / fb_s
        else:
            fallback = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
        return [float(x) for x in np.where(np.isfinite(fallback), np.clip(fallback, 0.0, 1000.0), 0.0)]

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
